2026-06-10 18:07:26 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Another thing I'll say is that marketing is THE biggest lever in the business.

Chatting is easy enough to get right.

Marketing is what makes the biggest difference. 

Most people trying to over optimize CTRs and CVRs and whatever else are mainly just not good enough at marketing.

Marketing is often gate kept because the people teaching it are still in the game. 

As much as it's good to have an "abundance mindset" if you share the UNIQUE elements of your marketing you have the potential to lose some money when people copy it and that's why people charge for courses, consulting etc. if they're still in the biz.

If I'm allowing someone to compete with me, I'd be foolish to not get compensated for it.

If they give out a free "strategy" on youtube that works, everyone uses it and then it no longer works. People see oh this girl likes outdoor stuff, that must be what works, then everyone does outdoor stuff because they're autistic and they diminish everyone else's marketing.

Marketing is actually where all the competition is.

The unique thing with Francis is that he's no longer in the agency side of the biz he's not creating competition for himself like most other people in the OF info space. Some of whom are still actually quite good and knowledgeable.

But he's literally practicing what he preaches, very much a 1-of-1 position in the OF space.

So learn from that, become 1-of-1, let others say "Oh they're copying X model" and if she's your model you've already won the game.

That was a bit much but I hope I'm getting the point across 😂.

2026-06-10 19:17:24 faceless francis -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
I think the other side of this is that a lot of the parts of marketing that are most gatekept are the formulaic parts. So for instance, if somebody figures out some sort of unique exploit or a working system with paid ads like Meta Ads, that system is likely to be infinitely reproducible, and those systems only work if other people are unaware of them. 

I mostly teach the contextual, qualitiative, psychological or even philosophical parts of marketing, so I can share more information, but if you don't already have an intuitive understanding of those principles it can be hard for someone new to implement it, which is where my consulting stuff comes in. The highest-leverage parts of organic marketing are pure context, so it helps to have someone with lots of experience prescribe unique, situational tactics or build out a strategy with a much higher likelihood of working on the first go rather than spending time and money on trial and error.

2026-06-11 11:45:59 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
- We have over 200 scripts (don’t do that it’s not necessary).

But I’d say you need 4-5 very long ones (10-12 sales) that get progressively more intense.

Then at least 10 shorter ones. 5-7 videos.

The more content you have though the more of it you can sell.

- When I managed more than one model it was all generic scripts. They wouldn’t say the same things but they would do the same things.

- Now that I manage one model we made new long style scripts that are niche specific and specific to her personality. Her vault had over 200 scripts before we implemented those niche specific, longer style ones.

- Different locations are good. Novelty is very important in this biz.

- Pricing ladder 100%.

2026-06-14 18:02:57 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
You’re correlating views with subs.

Which is not always the case.

I’ve seen people get 10M views on a reel and make less than $10k from it.

Why?

Because conversion happens mainly at the CONTENT level.

Super sexual content that gets tons of views doesn’t create higher revenues. Nor does it attract spenders.

Better to start with doing the right things instead of needing to pivot later on.

I have a 420k page that struggles right now to break 50k views per reel because the account was built the old way (thirst traps etc.)

Vs one that has 2k followers and does 20-50k views per post right now and will have a breakout once IG fully dials in the audience.

So better to just start content the right way.

If you follow trends, the account becomes a “trends” (random account) and doesn’t make much money. Then when you pivot it tanks. 

The best way to get out of the grey areas of Meta is to niche down and stay relatively safe with the content.

The sooner it can determine what your content is about the sooner it will get pushed.

Don’t look at the random exceptions, sometimes there is luck.

But if you want to reduce your reliance on luck make targeted content.

2026-06-17 06:27:47 5344814690 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
The idea that Instagram account “warmups” actually do anything useful seems, to me, like a piece of folklore that spread through marketing circles mainly because people kept repeating it, not because anyone proved it works. The whole practice assumes that the platform’s trust systems work like a biological process, as if an account needs time to mature before it can be trusted with more activity. That comparison falls apart pretty quickly once you think about how detection actually functions. Instagram isn’t rewarding patience. Its systems are built to flag unusual behavioral patterns, and a slow buildup toward suspicious activity is still suspicious activity, just spread out over more days.

2026-06-17 06:23:26 Shawn -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-06-17 06:28:46 edw -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
So do you think this is mid?

2026-06-17 12:18:05 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
When you start an account you need volume to quickly tell the algo where to push you.

The initial seed audience you get on a new account is around 300 people.

If the video gets stuck there it means the seed audience didn’t like it and it won’t push further.

But just do more volume the algo needs to learn about you.

It needs data and it analyzes the account on a rolling content basis.

It’s regularly looking at your most recent 10ish posts to determine what your account is about.

The more obvious the niche is the more easily it can push you.

2026-06-17 12:27:57 Rambo Robert -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
do you think after a while the algo just give up on you and decide not to push it because you suck ?

2026-06-17 12:28:13 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Yes.

I’ll tell you why/how.

2026-06-17 12:31:51 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
You get an initial seed audience.

Usually 500-800 people if you have a decent following.

If it does well with them it goes to about 2000 people, then further and further.

But if you flop with the seed audience and keep flopping then the audience gets smaller.

300-500 people.

Then 100-300 people like a new account.

So even if you have a BANGER it’s way harder to grow because those 300 people need to LOVE the video.

So if your account is stuck, high performing content can break you out of it but it’s still difficult.

2026-06-17 06:26:13 Shawn -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-06-17 20:41:54 faceless francis -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Just to be clear, wasn't picking on you in particular

I think your thinking here needs one minor tweak: content that converts is not content that is relatable to EVERYONE. It might result in high retention or even high views, but as you are finding, it doesn't convert very well.

You want to take the same approach, but be relatable to a narrow group of people to whom you can signal group allegiance and develop parasocial attachment

2026-06-17 20:45:56 5073923300 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
No problem, thanks for the clarification
. I actually mentioned above that I wanted to implement this idea I just needed to apply it to a specific niche. I think that's what makes it really powerful.
For example, imagine a fisherman sees my video where I'm casting a fishing rod, and the person standing behind me instinctively steps back to avoid getting hooked. He'll think, "Yeah, that actually happens to me all the time when I go fishing." At that point, he'll feel like, "This girl actually knows fishing."
That's the kind of relatable content that builds a connection with a specific audience.

When you have views and low link clicks or conversions that’s what’s happening.

2026-06-20 21:03:04 faceless francis -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
So, management in this business is really complicated and what you're describing is very difficult to find- VERY few agencies offer anything resembling true creative support in the way you're describing.

What most models in your situation do is just hire a chatting agency so they can focus on creative. But most chatting agencies won't take on a client that isn't yet earning at a certain level, because it will actually cost them money. So it's a bit of a catch-22.

I am going to be teaming up with Chatstar to launch a new agency in the next few weeks with the explicit goal of being basically the only agency that TRULY pairs peak creative with peak chatting. We're going to be ultra selective about who we work with, but as soon as applications open I'll post a link in here and you can put your name in the hat.

2026-06-25 03:42:56 CVP -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
What is the best monthly sub price to have?

2026-06-25 13:03:38 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
It’s not that relevant, you end up at the same revenue based on your traffic.

I’ve spoken about it before somewhere in the chat.

Search “sub price” or something.

I say with a paid page just make it easy to get in at $3 and then your renewal price is a reflection of brand. 

I’ve tested renewal prices at $9, $12, $20, $30 you end up with relatively the same revenue because churn increases with higher prices.

If it’s low the model may not be as “premium” which is fine. If it’s higher the model is going for a bit more premium, neither is better or worse because you end up at the same revenue from renewals based on traffic and churn.

I’d also not end prices is .99

It screams business not a girl who doesn’t fully know what she’s doing which is far more appealing.

Go for solid numbers especially for your sub price.

So the real question to answer is what does my renewal price SAY about the model?

2026-06-25 13:09:22 faceless francis -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
This is an excellent take on pricing. As Seth said, premium resub prices tend to maintain a higher LTV customer, but with commensurately greater churn. Understand your model's positioning in the market and tailor sub prices accordingly. A tatted, down to earth alt girl will not command the same premium as a sharply-dressed, classy corporate femdomme office siren type model

2026-06-26 17:53:48 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Ok let's use 300 as a number we can actually work with.

You have $2000 rev 300 subs, $6.66 LTV.

On a free page that's "ok." Not great but "ok."

==========

Your problem is bad content.

You're getting about 1% of people to visit the profile after viewing. 

If the creator is creating intrigue in a viewer you're going to get closer to 4-5% profile views.

==========

Then your link clicks were 3300.

Which is 6% of the profile views.

It should be closer to 10%.

==========

So you have a content issue and a chatting issue as well but much more a content issue.

Some metrics I look for to see if a piece of content is actually revenue generating is.

1) Demographics, majority USA etc.
2) 4-5% profile views to views ratio. = profiles views/views.
2) 10% link clicks from profile views. = link clicks/profile views.

Keep in mind some of these things tend to skew slightly lower when the creator is AI based.

==========

Now the second part is your actual conversion rate.

CONTENT is your biggest conversion lever. Not sub price. Not some crazy copywriting on the subscribe page. CONTENT, it's where the conversion decision is made.

You did 300 subs with 3300 link clicks, about 9% conversion rate.

If your content is doing it's job you should get AT LEAST above 12% on a free page but you can easily get much higher more like 15-20%.

So analyze your content and ask... why is this getting me views but not getting me money?

Views don't equal revenue like they used to 3 years ago.

Hey bro, I have a question about your approach to OFM and the way you teach people and create content for others.
Yesterday I was sitting down and talking with a colleague about branding, and we noticed that people who follow you and actively apply what you talk about, including us, have one mental conflict.
Let’s take the example of some random goth model. So, we place her in that niche, but since not every model is S+ tier, and besides being goth she may not have any real hobbies or anything else that could help us niche down further, I believe that people who know girls in real life and have direct access to them have a big advantage compared to models who are onboarded online.

2026-06-28 15:10:32 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
There is an element of “X Factor” that some people have and others don’t.

I come from the music business and you could tell that some people just have an “IT” factor. 

I’ve seen it with people who are stars and those who became stars. Sometimes you do get surprises though.

That’s doesn’t mean that people who don’t have that can’t do well, especially in an industry like Only Fans where the product has ridiculously high demand since it plays on human biology.

The key is to set your creator apart as much as possible.

Then look at the data to see where your bottlenecks are and then do your best to fix them.

IG in this model serves as a healthy middle funnel to top of funnel where we leverage the switch to interest graph they made from the previous social graph. Instagram is already moving to be streamable  TV content and testing series types out in certain markets. I view the more volatile strategies as coming to the end their lifespan on Meta, but I could be wrong. 
2-3 years ago we had 100's of phones, every gmail and platform account made from the same IP for each cluster, seasoned for 30+ days, sim systems spamming tf out of X and Reddit, pushing everything down the throat of IG and we lose an account, pay get it right back and have 10 more behind it.

 X links get 1:1 clicks on average to OF links for our girls. Which is why using it as a landing pad has been an easy decision for a lot of accounts. They also open sourced their codebase for the algorithm and its on Github. The X algo allows for emergent topical vectors that IG could never.
We root the foundation in Outbound cadence (substantive, value-adding comments/replies on other accounts) which carry high foundational weight on X and are completely independent of posting and visual post velocity. This is primarily through GraphJet/UTEG (interaction graph traversals and social proofs), RealGraph (user-user interaction likelihood with EWMA decay), and SimClusters (which refine your "Interested- In" vector across overlapping communities). It seeds discovery (especially out-of-network via "people you follow engaged here"), builds long-term authority, and supports emergent intersections by pulling your signals into multiple clusters. Our Outbound strat has up to 120 comment actions per day manually done by the model especially ones that lean into higher content safety tiers that have high visual post velocity. This helps pull embeddings toward healthier intersections and generates social proofs that amplify her own posts via GraphJet. The fact they open sourced their algo and no one talks about is absolutely insane. Its cracked if you put in the work, but you need devs or coding experience to really maximize. 

Everything strategy wise is contingent off of your models, your resources, your intended outcome, and the game you're playing. We made the decision to back off in high velocity marketing and invest infrastructurally including our team and technology.

2026-07-01 16:25:43 faceless francis -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Simplest explanation: think of niche as an interest or hobby that's small enough to be a category within a category. 

So "sports" would be a category. "Recreational golf" would be a niche within that category. 

Brand is the unique qualities that differentiate your creator from others within her niche.

So there are maybe a few thousand recreational golf content creators- what about your creator's personality, expertise, appearance etc. make her unique within the recreational golf niche?

2026-07-02 09:09:15 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
It ties to the root of the interest graph that Instagram is, the major Machine Learning models we know about (they have 1000 in total), and what that is and means

2026-07-02 09:14:58 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
"Graph" refers to the embedding space in which every account and everything inside IG exist as vectors. "IG2VEC" is an an iteration of Word2vec. A vector is a mathematical computation that ties everything about your account into, in Meta's case, a vector can be 150 dimensions. Try to take that in for a second. A line is 1 dimension. 150 coordinates mapped into a single vector existing in a space of billions of vectors

2026-07-02 09:15:01 8567261754 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
What you’re saying is extremely interesting, because maybe we are all stuck optimizing around local minima. And what you propose is very hard to test besides being risky, so that would explain why no one (that I know) is doing that.

2026-07-02 09:49:33 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
I totally agree. I'm 37, and have been in the game for years. My background was finance and in college kinesiology. I try to identify the substrate of things, and am absolutely obsessed with data. Data analysis is a spectrum and an art in itself. It is raw, and it is up to the individual to interpret. 

I'm also in the position currently to create a thesis that might take me months to work on and then deploy it across 20+ models. We operate everything in-house from front to back:
- chatting
- training
- operations
- finance
- social media
- software/ script/ app development
- everything in-house
We moved away from the model of collecting a certain percentage for marketing and also only work with certain creators that are usually more established. We can be more selective. 

I personally rely purely on data analysis, experience, and my intuition. I do think that people put a lot of emphasis on the minutiae of the details without actually stepping back and defining what their actual intention is with what they're doing. 

To me I haven't found it to be a big risk. I personally look at it as long-term. It's more of a risk to be associated with adult adjacency for a majority of my creators given Meta's alignment. Now there are absolutely exceptions to that and that's why I get back to it: it all matters on what your funnel system looks like. If your sole funnel is Instagram to be your bottom, middle, top of funnel and everything in between, I think that already is a handicap. The issue and concern is not getting flagged or banned. For me, that isn't an issue at all.

The issue is the audience demographic that I'll be relegated to for models that are of high value. Those models should be easily exposed to a broad audience and would be more convertible if they were exposed, reach-wise, ten times more than they're going to end up being isolated to by being vectorized in an adult-adjacent suggestive zone that does not align with the majority of the audience that exists potentially on Instagram. 

For me personally, this comes from the internal data collection on OnlyFans. I currently have 11 million rows of message data and 15 million rows of data points in an SQL library that I can query at any time and run a ton of different query scenarios and scripts. Everything is Python scripted to triangulate specific things.

I run this nightly. Every night I am ingesting everything from that day: my entire team's performance individually, every message that was sent, pushed out, unsent, everything. The next day all the "marts" I've created are set up and the scripts run. From there I can query and identify within them anything I need to see. 

The byproduct of all that is a thesis that I've been working on now for a few months. It's really the anatomy or anomalous funnel of the whale. That's essentially what I call it.

It is what is the framework and the psychological correlation of the whale or spender. Across the board I would say, composition-wise, across all of our accounts, ten spenders, ten people, make up 50% of the revenue every time. A lot of the time it's even more heavily leaning into the minutiae of the audience. If that's the case then what are we really targeting? Why do I need 10 million fucking views? What I need is to not be associated with an audience that is a low-spending demographic. Men who spend most of their time following creators are the worst kind of client. If you map that to a vectorized system, ALF and IG2Vec on an account level will tell you that topical authority is actually dictated predominantly by audience composition and behavior. 

To me it's lower risk to align myself on Instagram specifically with the audience that I want to attract. I don't need to hyper-sexualize because the models that I am using for this are attractive enough to carry their own weight. They're even more attractive when they're in their element doing something that engages them, whether that be exercising or talking about some stupid shit they like.

Whatever it is, it's actually more subconsciously attractive and of higher value to observe a woman who outwardly projects self respect and competency . It appeals to the higher resonance of the male mind. If you just talk about primal, visceral, low-vibrational carnal attraction, sure, seeing ass and titties appeals to that low-vibrational space but the low substance, sexualized creator is followed by an audience specifically and is engaged with predominantly by an audience that is low-value hedonistic, lazy fucking people that do not make up the ideal composition of a spender or of a whale. In a Social Graph model this did not affect the account at all like this. It was actually inverse: your followers were one of the heaviest-weighted pieces of initial velocity and every like by your connected distribution (which is your follower base) was a high-ranking signal to send you to the Explore page. That worked wonderfully then.

2026-07-02 11:11:40 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Well you have to try and evaluate the current vectorization and know your current audience would tell you where you're clustered. Also their engagement and who they follow can tell you a lot about where you're vectorized one of the easiest mechanical movers of vectors is actually through outbound engagement. Substantive engagement, let's say in the comment section somewhere that you want to be more aligned with an audience base, a composition that you want to be more aligned with, or a topic cluster that you want to be more aligned with, by spending a lot of time on that daily. That is probably one of the only real mechanical vector movers that you can do.

Posting: you don't have as much agency in the sense that you can post it but you can only control so much of the distribution process. So that comes with time. The more consistent you are at curating semantically aligned content, the more consistent it is with the topical identification. Then your signal starts to increase. You have greater coherency and a more coherent signal gets noticed by the distribution center much more easily because it's coming in much more clearly, much more loudly, and much more robustly. Your initial seed audience becomes an audience that is interested in the content you're putting out, which you've engineered to draw in the audience you were looking to reach at the beginning when you started that process. For a few weeks it might not mix. There'll be classification friction if it's in a different direction than your current audience graph but it will change. 

I would suggest registering as a Dev and getting the Meta Graph API for data

The idea would be to start with broad, neutral niche keywords, let the script discover public accounts/posts from those entry points, then recursively expand through mentions, collabs, tags, hashtags, audios, and repeated interactions. 2026-07-02 16:45:39 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
For the most part when it comes to the SEO side, I personally think that unless there are multiple creators with the same name, your creator is going to rank pretty high for their name specifically. That's usually where the google conversion  is coming from. 

Obviously there are sites like Babes Wiki and shit like that, but really they are going to come up organically if they're multi-platform, especially if they're YouTube-based. If not, I would definitely just secure the domain and spend a few months heavily focused on backlinks and blogs and bullshit linking to it. That will be more than enough because there can't be that many keywords that outrank your creator's name. 

We've been using X as a landing pad that holds their link aggregator, but will probably do custom hosted domains since that becomes the 3rd hop for a crawler to make which it wont follow through X . We've eliminated link aggregators for them on IG. When you look at their Link.Me, it was literally one-to-one: for every click on OnlyFans, there was a click on X , this made sense because the consumer is already assuming that there's going to be something more on X as it is. It's kind of a natural function already and it's been somewhat lossless. I mean there has not been a noticeable difference at all.

With that being said, we are in the process that I call the foundation. I used to call it sanitation but that was a little aggressive. Ultimately it's a 30 to 60 day commitment and it's not for every creator. There are some, like I had said previously, that I want to keep running the sexualized play on Instagram because our top of funnel is elsewhere. I just don't want any flags or bans but other than that I wanna play the game.

For a lot of girls, especially if you have YouTube creators or girls that are more experienced or have a higher-end feel to them, I think Instagram is now a really viable discovery engine for non-over-sexualized creators where you don't have to fight the system and the distribution of the interest graph and its organic nature with really strong topical alignment. I think it's honestly fantastic as a healthy middle funnel to top of funnel. If you think a super strong middle funnel is essentially the area where people get to know you, see you, see your life, connect, trust that you're real, all those things come with that middle funnel. From there they're either going to google search or they're going to go to X and convert that way. That's the philosophy at least that we're operating on when we deploy that and so far so good. The caveat to that is that what's unclear is how crystallized a vector becomes from historical account behavior and interaction. This is an ongoing experiment and my guess against it is that, originally, I was concerned. I thought that there were enough accounts that we should scrap, like accounts with hundreds of thousands of followers, and my original plan was that we'll go for 30 days but we're probably going to have to scrap these accounts.

What shifted my opinion was that I went back and actually reread one of the original papers I read that I can also send you. It's on meta-engineering and I'll find it. They categorize everything by year so it gets a little murky, but I went back and reread it and really reread how they outline topical authority and its effect. It led me to be more optimistic that, unless there are recent heavy flags, a serious history of violations, loss of the account, and getting it back, I feel pretty confident that any account can be re-embedded in a super clean vector space as long as they have a consistent output of quality content that aligns with the top, like a normal creator. You have to be putting out good content and what that looks like is going to be dependent on the space that it's in. This is a deployment that I use with girls. For instance, we have a girl who's crazy fucking fit, actually competed, is now retired but still does CrossFit, lives a super healthy life, and is also a Dommie Mommie persona, which lives on X and its graphic haha. 

Instead of showing that side, we just show the true fitness lifestyle and the more vulnerable exploration of what it's like to retire from bodybuilding, etc.  Even just an average lifestyle girl. It's like turning them into  a pseudo-influencer. Heavier with the day-in-my-life format, trying to have some kind of stronger anchor with those types of girls if possible. Sometimes there are just those girls that are hot and just don't have much else going on but their daily life. If they're comfortable talking, then you deploy that register and maybe take advantage of their comfort there. They might do some lives or show their personality by being kind of a troll. This is just my working theory but it is deployed and is continuing to. I'm not saying do this. I'm just sharing. 

It's not that the link in bio is some crazy thing that's going to get you banned. It's just that last thread that connects you to adult adjacency and inherently that is going to keep you further out of reach from a broader demographic than it would without it. I can't quantify the actual effect because there just isn't enough longitudinal data on it.

I will privately send you a great example of a girl. She, on her own, not at our direction. It was probably, when I say, six it's probably fucking eight months ago. I think it's because she was using Linktree at the time and she got a flag for just Linktree specifically when Linktree was getting hit. It was, I think, after all my links got hit. She just said, "Fuck it, I'm just never gonna do it again," and she put my name, the same name everywhere in her bio. I'll send you who she is, you'll see her look, and I'll tell you her revenue. You'll be surprised, man. Her traffic has never been better. The business is broken down into:

1. Acquisition
2. Sales (Chatting)
3. Marketing

See what stage you’re at and learn accordingly.

2026-07-06 14:12:43 KT -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Hey everyone! 
Quick question: how do you handle changing the subscription price on an OnlyFans account? I have a creator with a fixed standard price of $10. I’ve heard from several sources that it’s smarter to raise the subscription price to $30 and offer an 80% discount for the first months, because a certain percentage of the auto renewals stay on automatically and don’t get turned off. 

Then in the second month it hits them. How do you handle situations like this? Are there best practices? How do fans who were already subscribed at the old price react? Is it cancelled? Are the auto renewals turned off, and how does that work for fans who are already subscribed? Thanks to everyone for their expertise. 

If you have a different opinion, approach, or expertise on pricing OnlyFans creator accounts, please share it with me.

2026-07-06 14:17:45 jayd -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
you should ask seth

2026-07-06 14:19:04 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Every fan needs to opt-in to price change.

Some just agree.

There’s no automatic percentage of renewals that stay on.

The first month you’ll lose a lot of renewals.

The next month it will pick back up based on your traffic.

The overall money you make from renewals isn’t going to change much unless your traffic increases.

2026-07-06 14:20:54 KT -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-07-06 20:02:13 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
This actually touching on a deeper thread imo. Churn is inherent no matter how good your chatting and management is. Ultimately 1% of your subs make up most of a models revenue. The utility of a VIP page isn't specifically reoccurring revenue; it's to operate as a baseline gate. Even with a top-zero 1% model, subscriptions are always 9.99 and it's always fucking 70% off. If you adjust your subscription price, it also kicks everybody off rebill just a side note. The name of the game is finding and identifying the spenders, which are the small majority of people. Traffic is by far the only way to consistently overcome the renewal churn. Effective chatting strategy should be operating at a ratio of a bare minimum of $1 of subscription revenue to 7-8 (hopefully 10-12 or more) in tips and messages.

2026-07-07 18:09:54 Esoterikizm -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-07-07 18:10:02 Esoterikizm -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-07-07 19:06:19 1899875274 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
They are strict for the first video submission. Not only do you need a separate SFW/OFTV page you have to follow their submission guidelines to a T. The lighting, sound, quality and story line (beginning, middle and end) of the video have to match their specs. If anything is off they won’t ever tell you, they just won’t schedule you for an interview. 

If you follow their guidelines then you’ll get an interview. Interview process is just a quick 15 min zoom call for questions and what genre you want your channel to be in. Then your first video goes live and you can submit up weekly, bi weekly or monthly after that.

2026-07-07 18:11:41 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

Ie. I need someone who will actually work 😂.

2026-07-07 18:42:23 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-07-08 17:39:41 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
look into buying phone substrates meaning the raw pcb/ "motherboards". That's by far the best because you  shed the bullshit, and build server boxes. Its the most sustainable way to to continue with unique fingerprint hardware as well. Truthfully this type of system was something that, to me, was far more prevalent many years ago as far as just phone pharming in general. If you're actually trying to get into it today you're facing years of development on the platform side, trying to stop us from doing this. I was doing this three+ years ago. 

By harvesting the raw PCB motherboards, you're able to swap hardware devices more easily, with less friction and a lot less cost, because they will get burned and it isn't the fucking IP address. Let's assume you're actually sophisticated and you understand all of the nuance that goes into what's being tracked outside of an IP address, which is which is incredibly robust and if you think an anti-detect browser is stopping it, unfortunately you might be misled. At best you can just slow down the process, which is fine.
Something's going to leak. When it does, you need to be able to replace it immediately. Now it's much easier to maintain 100+ individual hardware units if you're using custom-made server boxes  if you're just using og PCB boards, which is all a cell phone is. A cell phone is a mini computer with a cellular networking card.

2026-07-08 17:38:26 KT -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Quick question: I set up my creator’s secondary account on a new phone and also created a completely new Gmail account on that device. Can I log into that Gmail account on my PC, or will that trigger anything? In principle, my Gmail account isn’t directly linked to my IG account.

2026-07-08 18:10:18 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
It wouldn't essentially trigger something. The issue is that if there's ever an issue in the future about trust score or any violation or anything that happens on the account, that's when everything comes crashing down a lot of the time. That's why maintaining operational hygiene is critical. A sloppy foundation  falls apart in the future. The answer would be: could you log into it? Yes. However you first need to understand what mechanisms you have to take into account, what components exist as far as trackability, and what it even means. I would also ask you: why do you want to log into it on your PC? You have the phone. The moment you log in on your PC gmail, your entire Wi-Fi network, MAC address, hardware IDs, everything associated with it, plus all the metadata associated with anything on your PC, becomes a security risk as far as correlation and relationship. If you want to use your PC as a hub of operations, then it needs to be isolated environmentally from everything else that you're also running. Make sure that phone is not ever touching fucking Wi-Fi.  At bare minimum use a container environment  and then an anti detect browser that's routing through a high end stable residential proxy. If you're running only 10 creators or so why even risk the cross contamination. 

Ultimately you're stepping into a world and starting to play a game that billion-dollar industries have been trying to stop people who are much more educated in this field for years. That's the baseline. You have to start by educating yourself and understanding what the fuck is even going on. Examine  the root of your question and you will start finding probabilities not an "answer".

If you're questioning whether you should log into the Gmail or not, first find out why you should or why you shouldn't. What are the possible mechanistic triggers that could negatively affect you at some point? Instead of just asking people yes or no binary questions, research and find out the mechanistic properties that make up the composition of the systems that you're working with. Outline your SOP and operating guidelines defined by critical thinking, disciplined  research, and  then apply sound logic and reasoning. That becomes the first SOP. The rest is the ongoing experiment. That's why disciplined operating procedures are crucial. This is all an experiment. Everything you will ever do like this will always be an experiment. Treat it as such. Create a thesis. Hypothesize. Objectively observe and document everything. Adjust accordingly.

2026-07-10 18:22:02 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Seth referenced follower weight being not as important and that couldn't be more true. The previous version of Instagram was a social graph and followers were one of the heaviest ranking weights you could have. Thus follower count plus likes created initial velocity to the explore page = cascading distribution. 

Essentially view each signal or decision-making ML as a mathematical equation. Now shifting to an interest graph model, the initial velocity rankers become completely different. 
Essentially I perceive it as a four-gate system. It seems:
1. Gate 1: your seed audience. At any given time there are billions of pieces of media floating around and retrieval has to be dictated by a series of pre-indicators before you even are shown to a seed audience. That begins with the semantic alignment of your profile, your trust score, the topical authority or coherence of that profile's niche or topic (Look into Cosine Similarity). There's obviously going to be a safety classification mechanism on the profile level itself, coming off a flag. You might be recommendable but there's probably an asterisk.  The actual Recovery Path Post flag is a toss up but could easily be 4 weeks on its own. Retrieval basically consists of the first portion. Now comes the other machine learning mechanisms before you even hit distribution. Before that fucking piece of content ever hits a screen, this is all taken into account plus now you have the visual side, which is FLAVA + IMAGEBIND, audio where ASR is used, on-screen text using a form of Rosetta OCR, and the text in the caption via NLP embedding. All of this now enters a fusion process to create a classification outcome and then you enter the distribution cycle. The beginning of the distribution cycle is pre-seeding. Pre-seeding essentially takes billions of candidates and seeds them based on the signals or coherency of the previous retrieval system. That could be considered gate one. This is where you're shown primarily to non-followers and it's a small seed system. This gate happens to actually be probably one of the most important. The calculations really come more so at gate three, and for our industry gate four is actually one of the biggest factors.

Initial seeding: if you have, let's say, a 60% skip rate, you're probably not going to make it very far. That might not be because the content sucks. It could be because the content is fucking just not right. A lot of times it's because of coherence. You're not being shown or distributed to the correct audience, or you're not allowed to be distributed to a certain audience. Gate two consists of some kind of follower showing and is less important. Gate three is the next sequence, which is where the computation really takes place. They haven't released the exact equation but you were pretty close in being able to dissect how it's structured.

You're going to take the highest-ranking signals, which are always going to be variable times X, the next variable is time x. Each variable carries a distinctive weight as well, which is unknown. We know that a low skip rate, high completion rate, and high hook rate (or a lack of skip rate) are some of the predominant factors as is initial share rate in the initial velocity signal so these are going to carry a heavier weight in this equation. Every single positive metric is going to be within that equation and at the end you have classification signals which are inverse negative factors. Something like this: 
EV = W_send × P(send) + W_save × P(save) + W_watch × P(extended_watch)
     + W_like × P(like) + W_follow × P(follow) + W_utis × UTIS_score
     − W_see_less × P(see_less)  ← only subtracted term 

Now for most people, that is the entire retrieval system that is going to dictate distribution and the likelihood of the cascade expansion continuing or the velocity towards the explore page in layman's terms.

Now we face in this industry what's called a Classification Safety Index. Everyone faces it but we have to actually deal with it. Let's say you're literally jumping out of the gym on all these other classifiers. Everything else is on point. Guess what? If you unfortunately get a CSI rating that is too low, you're not going to get flagged. No one's going to tell you. You're just going to get what's basically the heaviest negative signal you possibly can because it's a gating signal.
Let's say, for instance, suggestive. That is not at all explicit. That's not a flag but now it completely changes the composition of what audience you can be shown to and therefore it can suppress reach significantly. 

All this sounds pretty pessimistic but the beauty of an interest graph model is that the windows are short. If you don't continue to stack negative markers consistently over time, you can recover a lot faster and regain reach on your next 10 to 12 posts, let's say.

Now this concept is not novel at all. It is not unique to Instagram in any sense. The only part of it that makes it unique to them is the way it's deployed. That's what makes X just as unique in the way they choose to deploy their machine learning vectorized graph system. They used to use GraphJet, Heavy Ranker and various other machine learning models. They have now recently switched to replacements ML's Phoenix, Grox, Thunder, etc. Observing the difference in the Grox system itself. It's over a thousand dimensions. It's incredible and it is one of the most fundamental parts of their ranking system and also how they classify content:
- NSFW soft
- NSFW hard
- NSFA (not safe for ads)
They even more diverse classification systems the way they choose to regulate suppression is much different because they're not an advertiser-pressure company. They're an infrastructure company. If you abide by the toggling rules for explicit content or not safe for work hard and toggle on the fucking 18 thing there, your suppression tax is exactly what it should be. You're going to be shown to the right audience not yeeted off the map.

2026-07-14 09:04:32 Rafael -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
I know the ltv is very good but I'm curious about the churn rate, if it can be improved or if its already optimized enough.

2026-07-14 09:14:18 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
That becomes an investigation. First you have to find out:
- What percentage of the churn clients spent past the $3 entrance fee
- How many of them hit $30, $50, or $100 thresholds. Find the top 10-15% of churned customers. Go back and re-read every single message to start finding out where the friction points are. What could be done to have stopped it? Then I would take a look at the people who came in, paid the entrance fee, spent whatever it is (let's say $20 or $15), and made a purchase or two. What do those conversations look like?
- Is it a lack of interest?
- Are the strategies you're implementing maybe not focused around parasocial connection?
- Are they getting hit with too many mass messages?
- Or are they the typical take a look around, buy a product in the store and skip out. 

There's not one answer. What I'm alluding to is building a robust audience composition and trying to understand the true makeup of how your page functions. If it's anchored by whales, what percentage of revenue is that? Are we talking about two to three people making up 60% of your revenue or a different number? These all really matter when you talk about relative percentages and try to quantify whether this 35% retention rate is healthy or not. How old is the account? If we're still in infancy (first 60 days, first 90 days), it's going to be really tough to say. You have to really dive into the top 10 fans. To start it all starts there:
- What is making the money on the page?
- Why are the top 10-15% of spenders that were part of the 65% churn?
- How long did they stay for?
- Are there signs of instantaneous velocity where they blast off and then they disappear. 
- What is the chatting page strategy rooted around? Is it a GFE-style page? Is it a parasocial, connecting, type of relationship-oriented, interdynamic? Is it a semi-power dynamic elitist where the model is extremely beautiful, like a model-type beauty?
- If you deeply analyze the chats of your best spenders that have left and your current spenders that are there, go back weeks and store all that data. Really comb over it and try to find the overlapping correlative markers:
- How are the handoff instructions between chatters?
- Is there an obvious tonality difference?
- Is there any break in the persona that's noticed by the customer?
- Is that a through line that you see show up in multiple places?
Just diving into the nuance: if the page is older and more mature, you have a lot more to go off of and a greater timeline.

2026-07-14 09:21:34 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:

2026-07-15 14:57:09 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
He gives knowledge and sells access + knowledge.

Most personal brand driven businesses sell access (not all). OF is a personal brand driven business.

So there’s actually more similarities than people would think.

Education based creator
===========

- Content
- Telegram channel (1st level of access)
- Course/Community (2nd level of access)
- 1:1 Consulting (3rd level of access)
- In Real Life event (4th level of access)
- Partnerships (5th level of access).

Only Fans Faceless Creator
===========

- Content
- Free Page (1st level of access)
- Paid Page (2nd level of access)
- Chatting (3rd level of access)
- Exclusive content in chats (4th level of access)
- Customs (5th level of access)
- Video Calls (6th level of access)
- Video Call showing face! (Huge access) 

We deem education on the OF business as valuable, it matters less to us about seeing the creators face.

But… value translates in different ways especially if you’re niched in well.

If there was a faceless creator who was very entrenched in the golfing category she would still do incredibly well because people who value the culture of golf would value her.

I’m not advocating for people to make their models all faceless just saying it’s still very profitable when niched and branded well.

2026-07-15 14:28:08 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
People have to confirm any subscription price change so you’ll lose some and gain some.

You’ll lose some because they’ll be reminded they had their rebill on and turn it off.

You gain some because it’s cheaper.

The numbers will even out based on your traffic over the next 30-60 days with any sub price change.

2026-07-16 21:07:49 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
It'll be just a different iteration of what it is now.

Volume vs. Quality.

How do bans effect you less? With volume.

But volume usually reduces quality.

How do you out perform volume? 

Quality because quality creates higher leverage per piece of content.

Agencies will always look for the easy way out but if you can make high quality content on IG it will still be very profitable.

This month IG is matching our youtube in terms of average spend per customer.

This is unheard of for us because YT normally generates 6-8x MORE than IG.

But our content wasn't high quality enough. 

Once we made a banger on IG (with less than 100K views I'd add) the stats are climbing up to Youtube.

2026-07-27 09:02:49 5847135171 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
depends on so many factors.. Is it a paid or free account, what kind of marketing, what does the OF profile looks like etc.

2026-07-27 10:04:12 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Views to sub is usually going to be less than 1%. Far less than 1%. Especially on IG.

Better to track landing page views to subscription.

There’s no “baseline,” everyone will differ.

We do 1-3% landing page to sub.

And we do 3-5% or higher once someone lands on the actual OF page.

This is for a paid page.

Your mileage will vary.

2026-07-29 22:56:32 Seth - 🔥🤫🟠 -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
I’ve also spoken about this before in here somewhere.

EVERY business has friction.

When you make the page free you have less friction at the start but more friction at the end which falls to the chatters.

When you have a paid page you have more friction at the start and less at the end. The friction falls to the creator.

So it’s a game of delayed gratification with a paid page, more immediate gratification with a free page.

Do you take the frustration early on or do you take the frustration later.

Free vs. Paid it’s really just a matter of where you decide you want the friction.

2026-07-29 22:58:13 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
Honestly I think the free leads to the paid and they become a harmonious funnel at some point. The free opens up the doors the whole time you're working on the vision for the future.

I was going to mention this next 😅.

You can take an email marketing approach.

Free page for nurturing.

Paid VIP page.

Essentially a direct marketing/email marketing approach.

The free page is a lead magnet.

2026-07-29 23:37:09 Alex -> Faceless Francis OFM Community:
I currently have a few free pages that don’t convert to VIP funnels yet. in the past, I mean, I’ve seen three pages easily crack 100,000 a month. A lot of of them definitely went live pretty frequently but very doable.

The model had a real intention with it and it functions really well though because it’s like a big enough magnet for people to get through the door, but it’s systematic in your access to even be able to chat. There’s a lot of ways to create gates and filtration systems on free pages and then they can be excellent.
At scale, we’ve just found that we perform really well with Free, Paige to VIP, funnels and slowly mature and train the fans that the free Paige doesn’t even have explicit content if the model does explicit, and slowly the price scales up
